                There appears to be no advantage to our continuing our
yearly review of captioned matter.

.              In 1958 we adopted the practice of once a year reviewing i

cemplaints received concerning existence cf flying saucers fer the

purpose of determining whether instructions in addition ttohose

already issued to the field were necessary. The lest memorandum

dated 10/2162 pointed out that our policy concerning unidentified

flying objects was to conduct no investigation whenreceived in

the field but the¢field should submit the facts immediately to
the Air Force. Our SACletters dated i1n949 and in 1952 emphasized

tthiemaeffourer-tmhenetirionnsedtrupoclitciyotnaonstdhitefdieoelsdneorteanpepecaer sthsatarayt .this           {/1

               During the p1a2st months we have received no complaints

regarding flying saucers and during the past 24 months only one

complaint was received, tfhaects             of which were imediately

referred    to the Air Force.
